#### ON3-20 — @dibratto — reel — hattip-linkedin-os: LinkedIn strategy as a Claude skill — TEST

- URL: https://www.instagram.com/reel/DfFFGpzxcEe/ · 584 likes · 2,239 comments · ON2 inventory position 95
- **1. WHAT IT IS:** The repo locator sits behind a "REPO" comment gate — **not engaged** (one line, per standing rule) — but the reel's own screen recording names it, and it was resolved this run: **hattip-linkedin-os**, a guided, local-first LinkedIn strategy system packaged as a **Claude skill**. Per the reel's walkthrough: it interviews you about your company, builds an ideal-buyer profile, positioning, and content buckets, sets a posting cadence, and generates your first 20 post ideas; on-screen commands include starting the strategy flow and planning the next month, with sample calendar and draft outputs. The creator's backing claims (ghostwrote 6,000 posts for 50+ founders; "100M+ impressions" of data; analytics screenshots climbing past 1.3M impressions) are the creator's, UNVERIFIED. Verified at the source: `ChristianDiBratto25/hattip-linkedin-os`, 42★, MIT, pushed 2026-10-29, described at the repo as a LinkedIn strategy and writing system for founders, uploaded to the Claude app as a skill.
- **2. WHERE TO FIND IT:** https://github.com/ChristianDiBratto25/hattip-linkedin-os
- **3. HOW TO USE IT:** Per the repo's own description: upload it to the Claude app as a skill, then run the interview flow; it produces the buyer profile, positioning, content buckets, cadence, and starter post ideas locally. Requirements: a Claude surface that accepts custom skills — Garrett's stack has exactly that pattern (his custom-skills directory).
- **4. WHICH SYSTEM IT PLUGS INTO:** **GSE Content OS + Kit/client services.** LinkedIn ghostwriting for founders is (a) a funnel-tier service Kit could sell to the founder/local-business segment it already targets, and (b) a method input for Garrett's own long-form authority content. A packaged interview→strategy→cadence framework is the piece his content stack doesn't currently have written down — and it arrives in his native format (a skill), not as a PDF to re-implement.
- **5. CREATIVE ANGLES:** The gate itself is the second lesson: 2,239 comments on a 584-like post — a ~4:1 comment-to-like ratio shows the gate is the distribution engine, not a leak in it; GSE's paywall doctrine (tease categories, never the pick) is the same mechanics pointed the other way. And the format is a third: a working consultant productized his method as an agent skill and gives it away as the lead magnet — that is a Kit offer shape in itself (a free skill that interviews a local business and outputs its content plan is a funnel artifact Garrett could ship under his own name).
- **6. RISKS AND LIMITS:** 42★ and brand-new; the method is unevaluated until run, and the impression claims behind it are unverified marketing. LinkedIn playbooks age with the platform's algorithm. MIT license is clean for adaptation.
- **7. FIRST TEST AND VERDICT:** Smallest test: load the skill, run the interview flow for GSE itself, and judge the 20 starter post ideas against Garrett's voice rules — one session, zero spend. **Verdict: TEST.**

#### E-42 (ON3-2) — @gittrend.io — reel — Simple Jev: any open model becomes a classifier — TEST

- URL: https://www.instagram.com/reel/DfHcHndiYoN/ · 245 likes · payload verified at primary source
- **1. WHAT IT IS:** Simple Jev (featherless-ai) turns a compatible open model into a structured classifier/scorer **without training a classifier head and without generating text**. The mechanism, from the project's own README (read in full): you send shared context ("state") plus a set of typed questions — choice, rubric score, or true/false (truth/support) — and the server reads the model's **next-token logits for each question** and constructs the JSON response from those scores. The model never writes the answer; the server builds it. The current implementation runs locally on Hugging Face Transformers + PyTorch, with shared request validation, versioned prompt instructions, and response scoring in a plain-Python `common/` folder so other inference backends can apply identical rules. A public demo API runs with **no login or key**: 2k-token context limit, rate-limited to 2 requests/second, currently serving a Gemma classifier (`featherless-ai/gemma-4-26B-A4B-classifier`) at `POST /v1/classifier`. The repo also ships an evaluation framework (JevBench scoring, text/decision and vision suites, resumable HTTP execution, raw-response audits).
- **2. WHERE TO FIND IT:** github.com/featherless-ai/simple-jev — **verified via the GitHub API this pass: 628★, Apache-2.0, pushed 2026-10-07**; docs/demos at simple-jev.featherless.ai; demo API at simple-jev-demo-api.featherless.ai/v1/. The reel is a screen recording of this exact README.
- **3. HOW TO USE IT:** Two paths, both from the README: (a) demo — `GET /v1/models` to list served models, then `POST /v1/classifier` with `{model, state, questions}` where each question carries a type, instructions, and criteria; (b) self-host — clone, run the server locally against any compatible HF model. No training data, no fine-tune, no API key on either path (self-host needs a GPU-class machine for a 26B model; the demo does not).
- **4. WHICH SYSTEM IT PLUGS INTO:** Two, precisely. (a) **Agent fleet infrastructure:** deterministic triage/routing classification over fleet-bus and sweep material — sort, score, and true/false-gate messages and finds with a local open model at zero marginal cost, in exactly the free-first routing doctrine (demo first, Kaggle GPU lane if self-hosting). (b) **The GSE judge/arbitration research line:** Jev is the arbitration family the Laya eval (run today, verdict KILL per its pre-registered bar) belongs to; this is that family's simplest reference implementation, with its scoring rules in plain Python and an eval harness attached — the natural next specimen for the standing audition the Laya instrument now provides, and the cheapest way to learn what a logits-classifier can and cannot judge before any bigger candidate runs.
- **5. CREATIVE ANGLES:** (a) Rubric-score questions turn it into a cheap grader for sweep dossiers themselves (does this find name a repo? a license? a first test?) — classification the consolidation passes currently do by hand. (b) True/false questions over pick-state summaries are a smoke test for gate-style judgments at zero training cost — explicitly a smoke test, not a gate; the Laya result is the standing warning about confident classifiers. (c) The versioned-prompt + shared-scoring-rules layout is a portability pattern his own signal pack could borrow: one scoring rulebook, many backends.
- **6. RISKS AND LIMITS:** Young project (628★, single-vendor); the demo's 2k-token context caps state size hard — real pick states would need chunking or self-hosting; logits access ties it to open models served through compatible stacks (no hosted-API models); classification quality is bounded by the base model and is unmeasured on Garrett's material — the Laya eval measured exactly this failure class (a classifier that ratifies instead of discriminating), so any Jev result inherits that burden of proof. License is clean (Apache-2.0).
- **7. FIRST TEST AND VERDICT:** Smallest real test, no spend: run the public demo API against 20 already-graded items from this sweep corpus (10 clear TEST/ADOPT-class finds, 10 clear CONTEXT-ONLY) with three typed questions (names-a-repo? has-a-first-test? verdict-class choice) and score it against the human verdicts already on record. If it cannot separate those, it is a routing toy, not a judge — and that answer costs one afternoon and zero dollars. **Verdict: TEST.**

#### E-50 (ON3-13) — @thehackernews — image — CISA KEV: GitLab RCE (CVSS 10.0) + Cisco firewall DoS — LEARN-ONLY

- URL: https://www.instagram.com/p/DfHQCIIvjmg/ · 87 likes · payload verified at the post's own link
- **1. WHAT IT IS:** A vulnerability-disclosure post: CISA has added two actively exploited flaws to the Known Exploited Vulnerabilities catalog. Verified in full at the article the post itself links (The Hacker News, Oct 30, read end-to-end this pass): **CVE-2026-97229 (CVSS 10.0)** — an integer-overflow in GitLab Community and Enterprise Editions letting an *unauthenticated* attacker execute arbitrary code via a specially crafted regular expression in a CI/CD configuration; and **CVE-2026-76418 (CVSS 8.6)** — an unchecked-return/NULL-dereference in Cisco Secure Firewall ASA and FTD software letting an unauthenticated remote attacker force a device reload (DoS). The GitLab flaw's exploitation is concrete: Alpine Linux disclosed that an actor probed its unpatched instance on Oct 20–21 with crafted CI configs, crashed the worker processes eight times, came "close to working," and the instance was treated as compromised and rebuilt from scratch. Federal agencies are directed to apply fixes by **November 1, 2026**.
- **2. WHERE TO FIND IT:** the post; the full article at thehackernews.com/2026/10/cisa-adds-actively-exploited-gitlab-rce.html (read this pass); CISA's KEV catalog as the underlying primary source.
- **3. HOW TO USE IT:** The operational content is a checklist question, not a tool: does anything in the fleet's reachable stack run self-hosted GitLab or Cisco ASA/FTD, and if so is it patched before Nov 1? Garrett's code lives on GitHub (hosted), and his CI runs there — so the expected answer is no exposure, but the question is cheap and the date is tomorrow.
- **4. WHICH SYSTEM IT PLUGS INTO:** Agent fleet infrastructure — security hygiene. The durable payload is the attack shape: **a CI/CD configuration file as an unauthenticated remote-code-execution surface**. His fleet's equivalent surfaces are the CI configs and agent instruction files in his own repos — the same class of file an attacker would craft if the platform were his.
- **5. CREATIVE ANGLES:** (a) Pair with ON2-8 (the compromised Claude Code extension, same account, same week): the feed has now delivered a two-item mini-brief on agent-era attack surfaces — supply-chain extension and CI config — which together make a tidy fleet-hardening checklist supplement to the AIGoat list produced overnight. (b) The Alpine response (treat as compromised, rebuild from scratch, verify the artifacts) is the correct incident pattern, worth having on record before it's needed.
- **6. RISKS AND LIMITS:** News, not a tool; the Nov 1 date binds federal agencies, not Garrett — it is used here as an urgency marker, not a compliance claim. No part of his stack was inspected in this pass, so "no exposure" is an expectation, not a finding.
- **7. FIRST TEST AND VERDICT:** The test is the checklist question itself, runnable by the fleet lane in minutes (inventory self-hosted GitLab / Cisco firewall presence; expect none). **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY** (with the inventory question handed to the fleet-hardening list).


#### E-51 (ON3-15) — @wxll.hx — reel — installing MediaPipe in TouchDesigner (torinmb/mediapipe-touchdesigner) — LEARN-ONLY

- URL: https://www.instagram.com/reel/DctYHaUuJvM/ · 4,385 likes · payload verified at primary source
- **1. WHAT IT IS:** A 60-second install tutorial (TD30 series, day 15) for **torinmb/mediapipe-touchdesigner**: a GPU-accelerated MediaPipe plugin that brings face, hand, pose, and object tracking into TouchDesigner as native data. The demonstrated flow is the whole mechanism: download the latest release from the GitHub repo, extract (the bundle ships a `MediaPipe TouchDesigner.toe` project plus example .tox components), open it — every tracking module is live on load, delete the ones you don't need — and read tracking values by connecting a Null CHOP to an output. **Verified via the GitHub API this pass: 2,853★, MIT license, created 2023-08, pushed 2026-10-14** (actively maintained, not a weekend repo).
- **2. WHERE TO FIND IT:** github.com/torinmb/mediapipe-touchdesigner (the reel navigates this exact repo; the creator credits @blankensmithing/@domscott.art in-caption).
- **3. HOW TO USE IT:** Download the release .toe, open in TouchDesigner, prune modules, wire CHOP outputs to whatever the tracking should drive (visuals, parameters, recording). Requirements as demonstrated: TouchDesigner and a webcam; GPU acceleration is the plugin's stated design.
- **4. WHICH SYSTEM IT PLUGS INTO:** GSE Content OS — the tooling half of the TouchDesigner live-visuals lane whose output half is E-23 (the same creator's day-29 soundtrack post). This is the inspectable, open-source, MIT-licensed route to gesture-driven visuals — the category of tooling relevant to Nova-adjacent interactive material — with the standing fence unchanged: real footage rules for sports content are not touched by anything here.
- **5. CREATIVE ANGLES:** (a) Hand/pose tracking as an *input device for telestration-style control* — driving a replay cursor or a drawing layer with a hand instead of a mouse is a production experiment this plugin makes cheap; it stays an experiment until footage rules are applied to the output. (b) The release-bundle shape (one .toe + deletable example components) is a good packaging pattern for his own TouchDesigner work if the lane grows. (c) Two posts from one creator in one pass (E-23 + E-51) document both halves of a working system — soundtrack and tracking — which is more useful than either alone.
- **6. RISKS AND LIMITS:** TouchDesigner itself is commercial software (free tier exists with resolution limits — not checked this pass); GPU acceleration implies a GPU for the full experience, and neither of Garrett's hosts has a real one — the Kaggle lane does not run TouchDesigner, so a local test waits on hardware or accepts CPU performance. Plugin maturity is good (2,853★, MIT, current pushes) but it is a single-maintainer project.
- **7. FIRST TEST AND VERDICT:** The smallest test — install the release on any TouchDesigner install and confirm hand-tracking values arrive on a Null CHOP — is blocked on the same GPU/host question as the rest of the lane, so no test is scheduled from this pass. **Verdict: LEARN-ONLY** (verified, cataloged with its constraint; pairs with E-23).

## GAP AUDIT — what was NOT read at content level, with counts

In Garrett's format: what was not read, not returned, not dossiered, not evaluated — and where the evidence lives. Nothing here was killed; every item below is cataloged with its closing step on record.

**Not classified / not dossiered (the big one):**
- **DM-share classification gap: CLOSED Oct 30, 2026.** The Stream A re-pull dossiered the backlog to **649 of 654**; the 298-item Oct 14 gap (Instagram provider HTTP 429 on the post endpoint from ~05:44 UTC, Oct 14) no longer stands. The 5 remaining shortcodes are deleted/private (provider empty-record signature) and are cataloged-unclosable — named in the Oct 30 gap update below.
- **49 of ~90 repos notated-not-evaluated** (Stream D residual; Stream D-2 owns them; the 17-repo Full-Court Press block is the largest cluster). 31 were deep-evaluated (15 full dossiers); 10 of the 90 are Beexly-owned.
- **Laya-as-GSE-gate eval: RUN Oct 30, 2026 — verdict KILL (pre-registered §5.2).** All 3,081 rows ruled on CPU via the `laya` PyPI package: 3,077 PASS / 4 REJECT / 0 ABSTAIN, median confidence 1.000; Tier A passed-set −0.0873 u/pick (bar +0.02), FPR 0.9961, ECE 0.407 — it ratifies, it does not gate. No shadow seat. The frozen set + validated scorer + CPU harness is now the standing audition for the next candidate (Simple Jev, S110). Report: `~/workspace/second-pass/laya-eval/RESULTS-2026-10-30.md`.

**Not returned by the provider (counted, closed as unreachable):**
- **43 saved posts** never returned (API claimed 310, returned 267) — Oct 13 mining.
- **4,220 liked posts** claimed by the API but never returned (14,998 claimed; 10,778 unique returned = the provider's proven ceiling, (corrected) from v1's ~14,498-unfetched framing).

**Sessions/artifacts lost or partial:**
